# Bug audit: 2026-10-26

Area: every source file under `packages/ui/src` at `272101543`, less the docs app, the tests, and the benchmarks. 24 scopes, 1,730 files, 212,160 lines, 275 claims. 224 findings stay open: 0 high, 26 medium, 198 low. 51 claims are refuted.

No finding is high. The medium rows come first in the Findings table and in the steps; the low rows are mostly traps that no consumer root builds, contract sentences that the code does not keep, and forced-colors or keyboard gaps on rare paths.

## Scope

The area is the shipped library: components, modules, primitives, providers, hooks, recipes, core, utilities, types, layouts, structure, and fonts. It leaves out `src/docs` (the docs app, which a separate audit covered on 2026-10-25 and which the verifiers read as a consumer root), `src/__tests__` and co-located tests (the proofs, a weaker consumer root), and `src/__benchmarks__` (an answer key). 1,266 of these files changed since the 2026-10-18 audit at `c09101e01`, so the audit read the whole area again, not a diff.

## Method

Intent sources: `CONVENTIONS.md` §3.6, §3.9, §3.10, §3.11, §4.3, §4.9, §5.3, §5.5, §5.8, §5.9, §5.10, §7.2, §7.3, §10.7, §10.8, §11.3, and §12.1; `packages/ui/REFERENCE.md` §2; the TSDoc, the comments, and the tests; and the ARIA Authoring Practices pattern of each role. Consumer roots: `apps/admin`, `apps/places`, `apps/picks`, `packages/ui/src/docs/pages`, `packages/ui/src/modules`, `packages/ui/src/layouts`, and the shipped defaults; the tests are the weaker root.

Prior-art digest: no open audit covers the area (the 2026-10-18 record was deleted in #2816). The caller built the digest from the project decision log: 19 owner decisions (ruled out) and 9 surfaced suspects. The same digest went to each verifier and to the overturn pass. No sweeper saw it.

Charlie chose the middle plan: a sweep of every scope, a blind verify of every claim (low ones too), an overturn of the top severity, and one merge pass. Each sweeper read every file of its list; each coverage echo matched the list sent. A script stripped the `evidence` block (the line numbers, the trace, the suspected severity and kind, and the fix idea) before the blind pass, and it checked that no field outside `evidence` held a line number. Each verifier judged the stripped claims of two scopes. No claim came back UNLOCATED, so none was issued again. No verifier opened a benchmark or the sweep evidence. No claim came back high, so the overturn pass took the medium findings. The merge pass then joined the groups across the twelve sheets and ordered the steps; it changed no verdict and no severity.

Deviations from the command: each agent wrote its return as a JSON file in the caller's scratchpad instead of returning text, so that a script, not a hand copy, moved the claims between the passes. The leads were not sent back to a sweep; they are listed under Surfaced, not judged. An account usage limit stopped eleven agents between 05:22 and 06:00 UTC; each one resumed from its own transcript, and no pass was cut short.

| Pass | In | Out |
|---|---|---|
| Sweep (24 agents) | 24 scopes, 1,730 files, 212,160 lines | 275 claims, 25 leads |
| Strip (script) | 275 claims | 275 claims with no evidence |
| Blind verify (12 agents, two scopes each) | 275 stripped claims | 199 CONFIRMED, 24 NARROWED, 1 RESTATED, 51 REFUTED |
| Overturn (1 agent) | 29 medium findings with the sweep evidence | 29 KEPT, 0 REFUTED; severity lowered on G2-C07, H1-C06, P1-C02 |
| Merge (1 agent) | 12 sheets | 29 groups, 172 steps, 50 questions |

## Mechanisms

Each entry is the specification for `bug-resolver`. The lines are quoted as the verifier read them at the audit commit.

### C2-C01

- File: `packages/ui/src/modules/chart/engine/chart-legend/legend.tsx` (ChartLegend)
- Claim: The +N chip and the two page buttons are extra Tab stops inside the toolbar, and the arrow keys never reach them. Tab moves through the toolbar control by control instead of leaving it, and an arrow-key user cannot reach the overflow chip or the page buttons from the switches.
- Mechanism:
  - `packages/ui/src/modules/chart/engine/chart-legend/legend.tsx:681-687 const onKeyDown = useA11yRoving(ref, { itemSelector: 'button[data-slot="chart-legend-item"], button[data-slot="chart-legend-reference"]', ... manageTabIndex: true,`
  - `packages/ui/src/modules/chart/engine/chart-legend/legend.tsx:732-737 <Button type="button" size="sm" variant="plain" aria-label={intl.formatMessage(chartMessages.previousEntries)}`
  - `packages/ui/src/modules/chart/engine/chart-legend/legend.tsx:792-797 <PopoverTrigger> <Button type="button" size="sm" variant="plain" aria-label={intl.formatMessage(chartMessages.showMore, { count: overflowCount })}`
  - `packages/ui/src/modules/chart/engine/chart-legend/legend.tsx:692-697 role: 'toolbar', ... onKeyDown,`
- Trigger: Verified: the +N chip and the page buttons render inside the role="toolbar" div but match no itemSelector, so the roving never sets their tabIndex to -1 and never moves focus to them. They stay native Tab stops inside the toolbar, and the arrows skip them.
- Documented intent: ChartLegend @remarks (legend.tsx:525-526): "The row is one Tab stop. The arrow keys rove between the switches (Home / End jump to the ends)". APG toolbar pattern: Tab moves into and out of the toolbar, the arrow keys move between its controls. The test packages/ui/src/__tests__/modules/bar-chart.test.tsx:865 "roves legend focus with the arrow keys as one tab stop" covers the uncapped row only.
- Reach: shipped. Shipped default: the frames pass the tier row cap, packages/ui/src/modules/chart/engine/chart-frame/cartesian.tsx:212 maxRows={chart.legendRows} and sector-chart/sector-chart.tsx:446 maxRows={policy.legendRows}. Site: packages/ui/src/docs/pages/modules/chart/pie/legend-placement.tsx:34 (four-entry legend placed top or bottom) and legend-placement.tsx:39 (BarChart) at a width where the row overflows. The page buttons need more than five series in a side panel; no root builds that.
- Severity: medium. A keyboard user meets extra Tab stops inside the toolbar and cannot arrow to the +N chip in normal use, but Tab still reaches it. Overturn pass: KEPT. No source makes the +N chip or the pager buttons deliberate Tab stops: the roving comment (legend.tsx:679 "Rove across the focusable controls (series switches and reference chips alike), sharing the one Tab stop the toolbar exposes") names only switches and chips, and chart-legend-pager-focus.test.tsx pins the pager's aria-disabled focus, not its tab order.
- Kind: bug
- Prior art: NONE (searched the digest for legend, toolbar, roving)

### C2-C03

- File: `packages/ui/src/modules/chart/engine/chart-legend/legend.tsx` (ChartLegend (toggleReference))
- Claim: All marks recede to the rule and stay receded after the finger lifts. No pointer rests on the chip and no visible focus holds it (iOS Safari does not focus a tapped button), so nothing clears the dim until some later legend pointer or focus event.
- Mechanism:
  - `packages/ui/src/modules/chart/engine/chart-legend/legend.tsx:328-333 onClick: () => onToggle(reference.index), // A tap is not a point, as on LegendSwitch. onPointerEnter: (event) => { if (event.pointerType !== 'touch') emphasis.point(reference.index) }, onPointerLeave: () => emphasis.point(null),`
  - `packages/ui/src/modules/chart/engine/chart-legend/legend.tsx:640-643 // A rule // that comes back recedes the marks to it, because the pointer or the keyboard // focus is still on its chip.`
  - `packages/ui/src/modules/chart/engine/chart-legend/legend.tsx:649 setReferenceActive(off ? index : null)`
  - `packages/ui/src/modules/chart/engine/chart-legend/legend-switch.tsx:63 const position = switches.findIndex((element) => element.matches(':focus-visible'))`
- Trigger: Verified by trace: a touch tap fires pointerleave after pointerup and before the click (Pointer Events order for a pointer with no hover; not run in a browser). The click then calls setReferenceActive(index) directly. No pointer rests on the chip and a tap leaves no :focus-visible, so nothing clears the recede. On a browser that focuses the tapped button, a later blur clears it through emphasis.sync; on iOS Safari no focus lands, so only a later legend event clears it.
- Documented intent: legend-switch.tsx:162: "A touch is a tap that toggles the entry, not a point." The comment at legend.tsx:641-643 rests the recede on a pointer or keyboard focus that a tap does not leave. The test packages/ui/src/__tests__/modules/legend-touch.test.tsx:78 "emphasizes nothing under a touch on a chart reference" covers pointerenter only, not the tap that toggles the rule back on.
- Reach: shipped. Shipped: packages/ui/src/docs/pages/modules/chart/reference-lines.tsx:11-16 (BarChart with legend and two references) on a touch screen; the frame wires the toggle at engine/chart-frame/cartesian.tsx:210 onToggleReference={chart.toggleReference}.
- Severity: medium. A touch user in normal use is left with every mark at a quarter opacity after a tap, and can recover only by a further legend tap or blur. Overturn pass: KEPT. The intent the toggle rests on is stated and fails for a tap: legend.tsx:641-643 "A rule that comes back recedes the marks to it, because the pointer or the keyboard focus is still on its chip." legend-touch.test.tsx:78-92 covers only the touch pointerenter, not the tap that toggles the rule back on; no guard clears the recede after the click.
- Kind: bug
- Prior art: NONE (R17 is the Tooltip component on touch, not the legend)

### C2-C17

- File: `packages/ui/src/modules/chart/engine/chart-legend/range-legend.tsx` (RangeTrack)
- Claim: Forced colors sets a gradient background-image to none, and the track has no outline or border. The slider track disappears, and only its labels (and a probe marker while one is live) stay visible.
- Mechanism:
  - `packages/ui/src/modules/chart/engine/chart-legend/range-legend.tsx:398-403 const gradient: CSSProperties = colorRange.length > 1 ? { backgroundImage: linear-gradient(...) } : { backgroundColor: colorRange[0] }`
  - `packages/ui/src/modules/chart/engine/chart-legend/range-legend.tsx:416-417 className={cn('relative', horizontal ? 'h-5 w-full' : 'w-5', k.focus)} style={gradient}`
- Trigger: Verified: the slider track paints only with a background image (or color) and carries no border, outline, or sen.forced entry. Under forced colors a non-url background image computes to none and a background color is replaced (CSS Color Adjustment spec; not run in a browser), so the track keeps no paint of its own.
- Documented intent: CONVENTIONS.md §5.10: "a part that shows a state or an edge only with a background or a box-shadow keeps a paint under forced colors ... A surface or a track takes `outline`."
- Reach: shipped. Shipped: packages/ui/src/docs/pages/modules/chart/choropleth/heatmap.tsx:16 legend="range" under Windows High Contrast.
- Severity: medium. A forced-colors user loses the visible slider track in normal use; the labels and the probe marker stay, so the value is still readable. Overturn pass: KEPT. RangeTrack paints only an inline gradient or background-color (range-legend.tsx:397-403) with k.focus (chart.ts:84 sen.focus.ring) as its only other class; no sen.forced entry, no chart-module forced-colors fallback for the track, and no exemption in forced-colors-indicators.test.tsx. CONVENTIONS.md:150 asks a track to take `outline`.
- Kind: bug
- Prior art: NONE

### C3-C02

- File: `packages/ui/src/modules/chart/sector-chart/sector-chart.tsx` (SectorChart (PieChart, DonutChart keyboard navigation))
- Claim: ArrowUp and ArrowDown do not move to another slice. The cursor stays on the same slice, and the live region announces that same slice again. Only ArrowLeft and ArrowRight walk the slices, and they stop at the ends.
- Mechanism:
  - `packages/ui/src/modules/chart/sector-chart/sector-chart.tsx:382 const focusPoints = data.map((_, index) => {`
  - `packages/ui/src/modules/chart/sector-chart/sector-chart.tsx:385 return slice ? [slice.centroid] : []`
  - `packages/ui/src/modules/chart/sector-chart/sector-chart.tsx:456 focus={{ points: focusPoints, series: focusSeries }}`
  - `packages/ui/src/modules/chart/engine/chart-frame/frame.tsx:554 orientation ?? 'vertical',`
  - `packages/ui/src/modules/chart/engine/use-chart-keyboard.ts:274 case 'ArrowDown':`
  - `packages/ui/src/modules/chart/engine/use-chart-keyboard.ts:275 return vertical ? 'value+' : 'category+'`
  - `packages/ui/src/modules/chart/engine/use-chart-keyboard.ts:277 return vertical ? 'value-' : 'category-'`
  - `packages/ui/src/modules/chart/engine/use-chart-keyboard.ts:359 const next = stops[wrap(Math.max(rank, 0) + dir, stops.length)]`
  - `packages/ui/src/modules/chart/engine/use-chart-keyboard.ts:630 onRead?.(landed.index, landed.series, landed.stop)`
  - `packages/ui/src/modules/chart/engine/chart-frame/frame.tsx:537 if (text) announce(text)`
- Trigger: A keyboard user focuses the plot of a PieChart or DonutChart (tooltip on by default) and presses ArrowUp or ArrowDown after the first arrow placed the cursor. SectorChart passes no orientation, so the frame reads 'vertical' and the vertical arrows map to value steps. Each slice has one stop, so stepStop wraps to the same stop, and read() calls onRead, which announces the same slice again.
- Documented intent: pie-chart.tsx:22-23 "Focus the plot to read it by keyboard: the arrow keys walk the slices, the tooltip riding each centroid." donut-chart.tsx:59-60 "the same gaps, legend, tooltip, keyboard navigation". The engine documents the per-category stop model (use-chart-keyboard.ts:17 "a single centroid for a pie slice") but no source states that the vertical arrows are a no-op on a pie. Left/Right stopping at the ends is documented (use-chart-keyboard.ts:121 "clamped at the ends (no wrap)") and is not part of the defect.
- Reach: shipped. packages/ui/src/docs/pages/modules/chart/pie/animated.tsx:6, pie/segment-labels.tsx:6, pie/legend-placement.tsx:30, pie/no-labels.tsx:6, pie/callout-labels.tsx:6, donut/animated.tsx:6, donut/basic.tsx:6, donut/center-content.tsx:12, packages/ui/src/docs/pages/modules/dashboard/widgets.tsx:56. All use the default tooltip. Test reach: packages/ui/src/__tests__/modules/chart-keyboard.test.tsx:973 covers ArrowRight only.
- Severity: medium. Every shipped pie and donut fails the vertical arrows that a keyboard or screen-reader user meets in normal use and repeats the same announcement, and the user can recover with Left and Right. Overturn pass: KEPT. The engine model makes a one-stop category a no-op for the value arrows (use-chart-keyboard.ts:462 "The value axis arrows step the series' value points at a category in screen order"), but no source says the vertical arrows of a pie are inert; pie-chart.tsx:22-23 promises "the arrow keys walk the slices". The engine rule explains the mechanism, it does not make the pie result intended.
- Kind: bug
- Prior art: NONE (searched the digest for pie, donut, sector, arrow, keyboard). R2 covers plot readout Escape only.

### G1-C03

- File: `packages/ui/src/modules/grid/grid-edit-inputs.tsx` (GridBooleanEditInput)
- Claim: The cell does not revert. The boolean editor attaches no key handler and ignores `cancel` and `managed`, so the changed value stays staged and commits with the row.
- Mechanism:
  - `packages/ui/src/modules/grid/grid-edit-inputs.tsx:145-151 GridBooleanEditInput destructures neither cancel nor managed`
  - `packages/ui/src/modules/grid/grid-edit-inputs.tsx:163-175 the Listbox has no onKeyDown (text and number editors pass onKeyDown={editorKeys({ cancel, managed })} at :92 and :122)`
  - `packages/ui/src/modules/grid/grid-edit-inputs.tsx:62 if (event.key !== 'Escape' || managed) return`
  - `packages/ui/src/modules/grid/use-grid-editing.ts:876 sessionKeys: managed ? sessionKeys : undefined`
- Trigger: Default consumer-owned session (`session` defaults to 'manual', grid-editing-types.ts:259), a row open, the user changes a yes/no cell and presses Escape on the closed listbox trigger. No handler reverts; the value stays staged.
- Documented intent: GridEditInputProps.managed grid-edit-inputs.tsx:37-38 "Under a consumer-owned session the editor keeps Escape, which reverts this cell". GridEditableConfig grid-editing-types.ts:174-175 "removing the row from the set saves its changed cells as one batch through `onCommit` (Escape reverts a cell)." The boolean TSDoc :140-142 says Escape reaches it "through the grid table's key surface", which exists only when managed.
- Reach: shipped. packages/ui/src/docs/pages/modules/grid/editing/editor-types.tsx:131 `editable={{ rows: editing, overlay: false, onCommit: () => {} }}` (manual session) with the boolean `done` column at :117-121 (inferred yes/no editor).
- Severity: medium. On a shipped docs grid, Escape fails to revert the yes/no cell while it reverts every other inferred editor; the user can pick the old value again. Overturn pass: KEPT. The boolean TSDoc grid-edit-inputs.tsx:140-142 routes Escape through "the grid table's key surface", which exists only when managed (use-grid-editing.ts:876 `sessionKeys: managed ? sessionKeys : undefined`); no other Escape handler in the grid module reverts a cell under a manual session.
- Kind: bug
- Prior art: NONE (searched digest for Escape, boolean, revert)

### G1-C06

- File: `packages/ui/src/modules/grid/engine/grid-range/tsv.ts` (unguardField)
- Claim: The pasted cell gets `=1` (or `@x`): the apostrophe that the user typed is removed. tsvField did not add a guard (the text does not start with a formula lead), but unguardField strips it because the rest of the text would get a guard.
- Mechanism:
  - `packages/ui/src/modules/grid/engine/grid-range/tsv.ts:13 const guarded = neutralizeFormula(value)`
  - `packages/ui/src/utilities/export-output.ts:36 if (FORMULA_LEAD.test(value)) return '${value}`
  - `packages/ui/src/modules/grid/engine/grid-range/tsv.ts:32-36 if (!text.startsWith("'")) return text; const rest = text.slice(1); return neutralizeFormula(rest) === text ? rest : text`
  - `packages/ui/src/modules/grid/engine/grid-edit-commit.ts:516 const text = unguardField(cell.text)`
  - `packages/ui/src/modules/grid/use-grid-cursor.tsx:374 toTsv(rangeText(cells, rowsRef.current, dataColumnsRef.current))`
- Trigger: Cell text `'=1` (or `'@x`). Copy: tsvField leaves it as `'=1` (no formula lead). Paste: unguardField sees neutralizeFormula('=1') === "'=1" and returns `=1`, dropping the typed apostrophe.
- Documented intent: unguardField TSDoc tsv.ts:27-29 "so a copy and a paste inside the grid give back the same text. An apostrophe that the guard did not add stays." Tests grid-range-engine.test.ts:169-177 cover `'=SUM(A1)`, `'-x`, `'quoted`, `'-5` and no apostrophe-plus-lead text that the guard did not add.
- Reach: shipped. packages/ui/src/docs/pages/modules/grid/editing/paste-and-fill.tsx:19-31 (range, managed, scope cell, text columns name/email from people.tsx:113-114); copy at use-grid-cursor.tsx:374, paste at grid-edit-commit.ts:516.
- Severity: medium. A shipped grid shows a changed value after an in-grid copy and paste; the user can retype it. Overturn pass: KEPT. The round-trip promise is explicit (tsv.ts:27-29 "An apostrophe that the guard did not add stays"), and grid-range-engine.test.ts:169-177 has no case for an apostrophe followed by a formula lead. The trace `'=1` -> copy `'=1` -> unguardField -> `=1` holds.
- Kind: bug
- Prior art: NONE (searched digest for paste, tsv, apostrophe, formula)

### G2-C01

- File: `packages/ui/src/modules/grid/engine/grid-menu-targeting.ts` (openKeyboardMenu)
- Claim: No grid menu opens; the browser's native menu shows. The cell and column menus that a right-click opens on the same row are not reachable from the keyboard.
- Mechanism:
  - `packages/ui/src/modules/grid/use-grid-roving.ts:105-106 const mode = navigable || virtualized ? 'none' : hasCellClick ? 'cell' : hasRowClick ? 'row' : 'none'`
  - `packages/ui/src/modules/grid/engine/grid-menu-targeting.ts:97-99 const cell = target.closest<HTMLElement>('td[data-grid-col], th[data-grid-col]'); if (!cell) return false`
  - `packages/ui/src/modules/grid/engine/grid-menu-targeting.ts:130-132 const grid = target.closest<HTMLElement>(GRID_ROLE); if (grid !== target) return`
  - `packages/ui/src/modules/grid/grid-context-menu.tsx:441-443 if (tryCellMenu(target, event, resolveItems, commit)) return; openKeyboardMenu(...)`
- Trigger: Grid with onRowClick, no navigable cursor, not virtualized (row roving), default context menus. Shift+F10 or the ContextMenu key on a focused row targets the `tr`: tryCellMenu finds no cell, openKeyboardMenu returns because the target is not the grid element, so the browser menu shows and the cell menu (Copy, Export) has no keyboard path.
- Documented intent: openKeyboardMenu TSDoc grid-menu-targeting.ts:111-114 "Handles a keyboard context menu (Shift+F10 / the ContextMenu key) ... (WCAG 2.1.1)." contextMenu default grid-data-types.ts:998 `{ column: true, cell: true }`.
- Reach: shipped. packages/ui/src/docs/pages/modules/grid/events/row-click.tsx:30 `<Grid columns={actionColumns} rows={people} getKey={(row) => row.id} onRowClick={pick} />` (row roving). Analog with the same focused-row target (virtualized static stop, not the stated roving mode): apps/places/src/components/index-sheet/index-sheet.tsx:182 onRowClick with virtualize.
- Severity: medium. A keyboard user on a shipped row-clickable grid cannot open the cell menu that a right-click opens. Overturn pass: KEPT. The strongest counter-source is grid-menu-targeting.ts:115 "No-ops when no cell is active" and :117 "Only an event on the grid element itself retargets"; the remark gives its reason as a right-click in a cell with no menu, and neither line waives the keyboard path of a row-roving grid that a right-click serves. Not a deliberate scope.
- Kind: bug
- Prior art: NONE. R12 concerns a nested grid cell under the cursor, not row roving.

### G3-C08

- File: `packages/ui/src/modules/grid/grid-toolbar.tsx` (GridToolbar (batch-action row))
- Claim: The batch-action Toolbar unmounts with the focused button inside it, and focus drops to the document body. The 'Clear filters' button in the same component moves focus before it unmounts; the batch row does not.
- Mechanism:
  - `packages/ui/src/modules/grid/grid-toolbar.tsx:180 const showBatch = Boolean(batchActions) && hasSelection`
  - `packages/ui/src/modules/grid/grid-toolbar.tsx:244-247 {showBatch && ( <Toolbar aria-label=...> {batchActions?.({ selection, setSelection })} </Toolbar> )}`
  - `packages/ui/src/modules/grid/grid-toolbar.tsx:201-205 onClick={() => { // Move focus before the clear unmounts this button. if (toolbarRef.current) clearFiltersFocusTarget(toolbarRef.current)?.focus() columnFilters?.clear() }}`
  - `packages/ui/src/modules/grid/grid-data.tsx:817 hasSelection={selection.size > 0}`
- Trigger: Keyboard user holds Space or Enter on a batch-action control that ends in setSelection(new Set()); the batch Toolbar unmounts in that commit with the focused control inside, and nothing moves focus first.
- Documented intent: grid-toolbar.tsx:119-122 clearFiltersFocusTarget TSDoc: 'The button unmounts in the commit that clears the filters, so the browser drops focus to `body` unless it moves first (WCAG 2.4.3).' The same component guards one unmount and not the other.
- Reach: shipped. packages/ui/src/docs/pages/modules/grid/selection/batch-actions.tsx:21-26 (HoldButton onHoldComplete calls setRows(...) then setSelection(new Set()); HoldButton takes Space/Enter, hold-button.tsx:142).
- Severity: medium. On a shipped docs surface a keyboard user loses focus to body after a batch action, and recovers by tabbing in again. Overturn pass: KEPT. The same component guards the sibling unmount (grid-toolbar.tsx:119-122 clearFiltersFocusTarget, WCAG 2.4.3) and the batchActions TSDoc (grid-data-types.ts:531-533) says nothing about focus, so the unguarded batch row is not a stated choice.
- Kind: bug
- Prior art: NONE (searched the digest for toolbar, batch, focus)

### G4-C02

- File: `packages/ui/src/modules/grid/engine/grid-row/shell.ts` (rowShellProps / buildRovingCellActivate)
- Claim: A keyboard user can focus the row or the cell, but Enter and Space do nothing: no keyboard route reaches the double-click action that a pointer can do.
- Mechanism:
  - `packages/ui/src/modules/grid/use-grid-roving.ts:108-113 const hasRowClick = onRowClick || onRowDoubleClick ... const mode = navigable || virtualized ? 'none' : hasCellClick ? 'cell' : hasRowClick ? 'row' : 'none'`
  - `packages/ui/src/modules/grid/engine/grid-row/shell.ts:153 onKeyDown: onRowClick ? ... : undefined,`
  - `packages/ui/src/modules/grid/engine/grid-row/bridges.ts:119 if (!handleCellClick && !handleRowClick) return undefined`
  - `packages/ui/src/modules/grid/use-grid-data-cursor.ts:274 () => buildRovingCellActivate(handleCellClick, handleRowClick),`
- Trigger: Non-navigable, non-virtualized grid with only onCellDoubleClick (cells rove) or only onRowDoubleClick (rows rove): the items take focus and the clickable focus ring, but cellActivate is undefined and the row keydown is absent, so Enter/Space do nothing and no keyboard route reaches the double-click action.
- Documented intent: shell.ts:76-78 rowClickableClass TSDoc: 'the pointer cursor and keyboard focus ring' for any of four handlers; use-grid-roving.ts:43-45 'A clickable grid (any row- or cell-level click handler) ... becomes a single Tab stop.' WCAG 2.1.1 Keyboard. Counter-note: grid-data-types.ts:1277-1279 onRowDoubleClick 'is a secondary "open" affordance layered over a primary onRowClick'.
- Reach: shipped. packages/ui/src/docs/pages/modules/grid/events/double-click.tsx:22 (onCellDoubleClick alone, cell roving). Row-mode variant (onRowDoubleClick alone): NONE in the roots.
- Severity: medium. On a shipped docs grid a keyboard user reaches a focusable cell that cannot run its only action; the TSDoc treats double-click as secondary, so production configs keep a primary path. Overturn pass: KEPT. utilities/modifier-keys.ts:10-11 "Such a chord is for the browser ... A handler must not take it." No KEPT entry in browser-modifier-boundary.test.ts covers menu-sub.tsx (the gate only finds hand-written chains), and no comment in menu-sub.tsx exempts the arrow handlers.

- Reach: shipped. apps/places/src/components/place-form-drawer/place-form-drawer.tsx:203 <Rating name="rating" size="lg" step={0.5} /> inside FormDrawer (form-drawer.tsx:137 <Form<V>, async onSubmit place-form-drawer.tsx:121); packages/ui/src/docs/pages/components/rating/in-a-form.tsx:21 <Rating name="score" /> with a 1 s async onSubmit.
- Severity: medium. On a shipped form the focused radio unmounts, focus falls to <body> and is not restored after the submit; the keyboard user recovers by tabbing back. Overturn pass: KEPT. Two documented rules meet: rating.tsx:165-166 "`readOnly` and `disabled` drop the radios and render one `role=\"img\"`" and the lock contract control/context.ts:64-65 "while each part keeps the focus" (also MODULES.md:19 "Nothing is disabled, so the focus stays where it is"). The lock rule is the one about focus, so the swap under a transient lock is not intended.
- Kind: bug
- Prior art: R3 ("Checkbox, Radio, and Rating native form reset already works") covers reset only, not the lock swap. NONE for focus under the lock.

### M2-C04

- File: `packages/ui/src/modules/map/map-points.tsx` (MapPoints (stop numbering by drawn group))
- Claim: The zoom regroups the set, so the stop index the cursor holds now names a different drawn group. The re-anchor effect moves the readout to that other dot or summary and the tooltip reads its name and detail, so the cursor jumps to a dot the reader never moved to. A pointer readout held across the regroup likewise names a different group, or falls back to the mark's label when the index is past the new group count.
- Mechanism:
  - `packages/ui/src/modules/map/map-points.tsx:366-367 const groups = useMemo( () => clusterPoints(positions, project, gap, unitsPerPixel),`
  - `packages/ui/src/modules/map/map-points.tsx:459-460 const stops = useCallback( () => groups.map((group) => clusterAnchor(group.members, positions)),`
  - `packages/ui/src/modules/map/use-map-keyboard.ts:38-39 return target.kind === 'region' ? r${target.index} : e${target.id}:${target.stop}`
  - `packages/ui/src/modules/map/use-map-keyboard.ts:212 cursor === null ? null : (resolved(moved).where.get(stopKey(cursor)) ?? null)`
  - `packages/ui/src/modules/map/use-map-keyboard.ts:294 const cursorStop = index === null ? null : (stops()[index] ?? null)`
  - `packages/ui/src/modules/map/use-map-keyboard.ts:307 anchor(cursorStop, transform)`
- Trigger: On a zooming plat, + or - (or a wheel or pinch) changes unitsPerPixel, MapPoints regroups, and a summary that splits shifts every later group index. The cursor's key `e{id}:{n}` still resolves to group n, now a different dot or summary, and the re-anchor effect moves the readout and tooltip onto it. A pointer readout held across the regroup likewise names group n of the new grouping.

### Q2-C01

- File: `packages/ui/src/modules/chat/chat-prompt.tsx` (ChatPrompt)
- Claim: The button that holds the focus turns into the send button with the native `disabled` attribute (the draft is empty), so the focus falls out of the composer to the document body. A keyboard user must find the composer again after each reply.
- Mechanism:
  - `packages/ui/src/modules/chat/chat-prompt.tsx:149 const canSubmit = !disabled && canSubmitDraft(value)`
  - `packages/ui/src/modules/chat/chat-prompt.tsx:220 {streaming ? (`
  - `packages/ui/src/modules/chat/chat-prompt.tsx:234 disabled={!canSubmit}`
  - `packages/ui/src/components/button/button.tsx:159 {...(props as Omit<ComponentProps<'button'>, 'className'>)}`
  - `packages/ui/src/modules/chat/use-chat-send.ts:300 setStreaming(true)`
  - `packages/ui/src/modules/chat/use-chat-draft.ts:51 setValue('')`
- Trigger: The stop and send arms of the ternary are the same element type at the same position, so React keeps one <button> node. useChatDraft.submit clears the draft and useChatSend.send sets streaming in the same batch, so the focused node becomes the enabled stop button. When the reply ends or the user stops it, streaming goes false, and the same focused node takes native `disabled` because the draft is empty. Chromium then moves the focus to <body> (probe in the scratchpad: activeElement is body after `disabled = true` on a focused button). Without streaming (the Default demo), the focus falls at the send click itself.
- Documented intent: chat-prompt.tsx:263 "Returns focus to the textarea after chip removal (WCAG 2.4.3)."; Button TSDoc button.tsx:39 "A loading button stays enabled, so it keeps the focus that it has." The test chat-prompt.test.tsx:156 'disables send when value is blank' pins the disabled state, not the focus loss; nothing documents the loss as intended.
- Reach: shipped. packages/ui/src/docs/pages/modules/chat/prompt/streaming.tsx:17 (stop -> streaming false, empty draft); packages/ui/src/docs/pages/modules/chat/prompt/default.tsx:7, with-actions.tsx:14, with-attachments.tsx (send clears the draft with no streaming, so the focus falls at once). No app root renders ChatPrompt.
- Severity: medium. A keyboard user on a shipped docs surface loses the focus to the body after each send or stop, and must find the composer again; the user can recover. Overturn pass: KEPT. No source accepts the focus loss: button.tsx:39 "A loading button stays enabled, so it keeps the focus that it has", and legend.tsx:730-731 states the repository's own reading that a native disabled "drops the focus to the body". The two arms of the ternary (chat-prompt.tsx:220-238) share one element slot, so the focused node takes `disabled`.
- Kind: bug
- Prior art: NONE (digest searched for chat, prompt, focus)

### C1-C03

- File: `packages/ui/src/modules/chart/engine/chart-time.ts` (dateCategoryFormat)
- Claim: On a V8 engine the axis relabels the categories as dates (`03/01/2024`); on an engine whose `Date.parse` rejects the text, the raw labels stay. The labels then differ between a Node server render and a non-V8 browser.
- Mechanism:
  - `packages/ui/src/modules/chart/engine/chart-time.ts:520 |\b(jan|feb|mar|apr|may|jun|jul|aug|sep|oct|nov|dec)[a-z]*\.?,?\s+\d|`
  - `packages/ui/src/modules/chart/engine/chart-time.ts:121 return numbers.length === 1 && (numbers[0]?.length ?? 0) <= 2`
  - `packages/ui/src/modules/chart/engine/chart-time.ts:166 const time = Date.parse(value)`
  - `packages/ui/src/modules/chart/engine/chart-time.ts:559 if (typeof value === 'string' && !DATE_SHAPED.test(value.trim())) return null`
- Trigger: Verified on V8 (node): Date.parse('Marketing 2024') gives 1 Mar 2024 and Date.parse('Decor 2023') gives 1 Dec 2023. The `[a-z]*` after the month prefix lets any word that starts with a month abbreviation match DATE_SHAPED; the four-digit number skips the yearless guard; dateCategoryFormat then relabels the axis as dates (03/01/2024 with the year, because 2024 is not the reference year). The non-V8 half (raw labels on an engine whose Date.parse refuses the text, and the SSR/browser divergence) is not verified here.
- Documented intent: DATE_SHAPED TSDoc (chart-time.ts:512-513): "A month name beside a day or year: `Jun 2026`, `10 June 2026`." dateCategoryFormat TSDoc (chart-time.ts:531-532): "Numbers and strings without a date shape (\"Region 3\") do not count either." 'Marketing' is not a month name.
- Reach: none. NONE. No consumer root passes month-prefixed word categories with a year; searched packages/ui/src/docs/pages/modules/chart/data.ts (month/week/source names) and docs/pages/modules/dashboard/widgets.tsx.

- Kind: bug
- Prior art: NONE

### C1-C06

- File: `packages/ui/src/modules/chart/engine/chart-geometry/bar.ts` (barMarks)
- Claim: Each bar falls back to a 1 px thickness, but the group still adds a 4 px gap between bars, so the group grows wider than its band and its outer bars overlap the bars of the neighboring categories. The bar hit test then reads overlapping spans.
- Mechanism:
  - `packages/ui/src/modules/chart/engine/chart-geometry/bar.ts:157 const slot = (band.width - (seriesCount - 1) * MARK_GAP) / seriesCount`
  - `packages/ui/src/modules/chart/engine/chart-geometry/bar.ts:159 const thickness = Math.max(1, thick ? slot : Math.min(BAR_MAX_WIDTH, slot))`
  - `packages/ui/src/modules/chart/engine/chart-geometry/bar.ts:161 const group = seriesCount * thickness + (seriesCount - 1) * MARK_GAP`
  - `packages/ui/src/modules/chart/engine/chart-geometry/bar.ts:169 const c0 = band.at(index) + (band.width - group) / 2 + seriesIndex * (thickness + MARK_GAP)`
  - `packages/ui/src/modules/chart/engine/chart-constants.ts:17 export const MARK_GAP = 4`
- Trigger: Verified: six series over thirty categories in a 400 px plot gives step 13.3 and band width 10.7 (padding 0.2). slot is negative, thickness clamps to 1, group is 6 + 20 = 26 px, so the group overhangs its band by about 7.7 px on each side and crosses the 2.7 px of air into the neighbor bands. Overflow needs band.width < seriesCount + (seriesCount - 1) * 4.
- Documented intent: barMarks TSDoc (bar.ts:134-136): "Each category's band splits into per-series bars capped at the spec thickness. They are separated by the surface gap, and centered as a group in their band."
- Reach: none. NONE. - Severity: low. Overlapping bars are a visible wrong result, but no consumer root builds enough series in narrow enough bands (R19).
- Kind: bug
- Prior art: NONE

- Claim: All labels are kept, but the band centers sit closer together than one label slot, so adjacent category labels touch or overlap (horizontally on a vertical chart; their line boxes stack closer than one line on a horizontal chart).
- Mechanism:
  - `packages/ui/src/modules/chart/engine/chart-layout.ts:150 const nth = Math.ceil(count / labelFit(axisLength, slot))`
  - `packages/ui/src/modules/chart/engine/chart-layout.ts:922-925 const band = bandScale({ count, range: markInsetRange(bandExtent('vertical', plot), getBandPad(input)), })`
  - `packages/ui/src/modules/chart/engine/chart-layout.ts:940 bandTicks: bandAxisTicks(input, band, plot.width, slot, bandMode, tilt),`
  - `packages/ui/src/modules/chart/engine/chart-layout.ts:1152-1156 bandTicks: bandAxisTicks( input, band, plot.height, BAND_LABEL_HEIGHT,`
  - `packages/ui/src/modules/chart/engine/chart-layout.ts:443 return input.axes ? BAND_EDGE_PAD : getMarkPad(input)`
  - `packages/ui/src/modules/chart/engine/chart-constants.ts:88 export const BAND_EDGE_PAD = 16`
- Trigger: Verified: thinned() fits labels against the full plot length, but the band centers spread over that length less 2 x BAND_EDGE_PAD (32 px). When the count sits exactly at the fit, the center step is up to 32/count px under the slot. Horizontal: twelve categories in a 200 px plot fit 12 slots of 16 px, but the step is (200 - 32)/12 = 14 px, so the 16 px line boxes overlap. Vertical: the slot holds 8 px of air, so labels overlap only when 32/count exceeds that air, which needs three or fewer categories and a plot width in a narrow window (for nine-character labels, about 253 to 261 px). The claim's 384 px vertical example lands in the window only with gutters of about 61 to 65 px each; not computed exactly.
- Documented intent: thinned TSDoc (chart-layout.ts:135-136): "The category indexes whose labels fit along an axis without colliding: every label when there is room, else every nth".
- Reach: shipped. Shipped default: every framed cartesian chart in thinned mode passes plot.width / plot.height (chart-layout.ts:940, 1152-1156). Sites that can meet the window: packages/ui/src/docs/pages/modules/dashboard/widgets.tsx:39 (RevenueBars, few categories in a resizable tile); docs/pages/modules/chart/playground.tsx:6 (BarChart with an orientation choice field, six categories). Whether a given site lands in the window depends on the measured frame.
- Severity: low. The overlap is at most 32/count px and only inside a narrow band of frame sizes (vertical: three or fewer categories), so labels touch rather than lose legibility.
- Kind: bug
- Prior art: NONE

### K5-C01

- File: `packages/ui/src/components/json-tree/json-tree-keyboard.ts` (windowTabStop)
- Claim: The Tab stop is on flat row 0, which is in the overscan above the viewport. Tab focuses that row, and the browser scrolls the tree back to its top instead of entering on the first row in view.
- Mechanism:
  - `packages/ui/src/components/json-tree/json-tree-keyboard.ts:107 const inView = first === 0 ? 0 : first + overscan`
  - `packages/ui/src/components/json-tree/json-tree-keyboard.ts:109 return indexes.find((index) => index >= inView && focusable(index)) ?? indexes.find(focusable)`
  - `packages/ui/node_modules/@tanstack/virtual-core/dist/esm/index.js:20 const start = Math.max(range.startIndex - range.overscan, 0);`
  - `packages/ui/src/components/json-tree/json-tree-virtualized.tsx:305 virtualItems.map((virtualItem) => virtualItem.index),`
  - `packages/ui/src/components/json-tree/json-tree-virtualized.tsx:461 tabbable={virtualItem.index === tabStop}`
- Trigger: Virtualized JsonTree scrolled so the first row in view has flat index v with 0 < v <= overscan. The window then starts at max(v - overscan, 0) = 0, so `first === 0` sets inView to 0 and the Tab stop goes to row 0, above the viewport. Tab from outside focuses it and the browser scrolls the tree to the top.
- Documented intent: json-tree.tsx:133-134 "Tab enters the tree on the first row in view." json-tree-keyboard.ts:84-85 "The stop is the first row in the viewport, or after it, that is not a closing bracket. Thus Tab does not move focus to a row above the viewport." Tests (json-tree-keyboard.test.ts:51) cover only range(0,5) where row 0 is in view.
- Reach: none. NONE. Searched apps/admin, apps/places, apps/picks, packages/ui/src/docs/pages, packages/ui/src/modules, packages/ui/src/layouts for `<JsonTree` and `virtualize`: no consumer passes `virtualize`. The docs playground (docs/pages/components/json-tree/playground.tsx:5) cannot set an object prop (docs/kit/fields.ts builds only choice, text, and number fields). Shipped default: `virtualize` is off by default.
- Severity: low. A wrong scroll on Tab entry that the user can recover from, but no consumer root builds a virtualized tree (R19).
- Kind: bug
- Prior art: NONE (searched digest for JsonTree, tab stop, windowTabStop, overscan)
- Dependency: @tanstack/virtual-core 3.17.11. pnpm-workspace.yaml overrides: esbuild, postcss, undici only; patchedDependencies: @floating-ui/react@0.27.20 and react-router@8.4.0 only; patches/ holds those two only; tsconfig.base.json paths: "@/*": ["./src/*"] only; no resolve.alias in packages/ui vite/vitest configs; no vendored copy (src/hooks/use-virtual-window.ts imports from '@tanstack/virtual-core' and sets no rangeExtractor). None applies.

### K5-C04

- File: `packages/ui/src/components/json-tree/json-tree-node.tsx` (JsonTreeNodeView (nodePath))
- Claim: The branches under the root stay closed. The recursive node path of a root child is `a`, while `collectJsonTreePaths`, the search seed, and the virtualized variant give `.a`, so no path in the set matches a node below the root.

  - `packages/ui/src/components/json-tree/json-tree-utilities.tsx:75 return ${parentPath}.${encodePathSegment(childKey)}`

### K5-C05

- File: `packages/ui/src/components/pdf-viewer/pdf-viewer-document-cache.ts` (ensureDocumentLoad (settle) / forgetFailure)
- Claim: The new viewer's first render reads the stale failed snapshot: it paints the "Failed to load PDF" alert, and its `onError` fires with the old error, before the retry that its effect starts. When the retry succeeds, `onLoad` also fires for the same src.
- Mechanism:
  - `packages/ui/src/components/pdf-viewer/pdf-viewer-document-cache.ts:290 if (documents.get(src) !== held || held.loading || held.listeners.size > 0) return`
  - `packages/ui/src/components/pdf-viewer/pdf-viewer-document-cache.ts:393-396 return () => { held.listeners.delete(listener) ... forgetFailure(src, held)`
  - `packages/ui/src/components/pdf-viewer/pdf-viewer-document-cache.ts:500-507 const settle = ... publish(src, { loading: false, ...next }) evict() (no forgetFailure after a failed settle)`
  - `packages/ui/src/components/pdf-viewer/pdf-viewer-document-cache.ts:266 if (documents.size <= MAX_DOCUMENTS) return`
  - `packages/ui/src/components/pdf-viewer/pdf-viewer-document-cache.ts:371 return documents.get(src)?.snapshot ?? EMPTY`
  - `packages/ui/src/components/pdf-viewer/use-pdf-viewer.ts:56 if (error) return error`
  - `packages/ui/src/components/pdf-viewer/use-pdf-viewer.ts:460 if (settled) notifyError(settled)`
  - `packages/ui/src/components/pdf-viewer/pdf-viewer-viewport.tsx:105-107 ) : error ? ( <div role="alert" ...> {intl.formatMessage(pdfViewerMessages.loadFailed, ...)}`
- Trigger: The last viewer on `src` unmounts while its load runs (forgetFailure returns on `held.loading`), then the load fails; settle publishes the error and never forgets it. A new viewer on the same `src` reads that snapshot in its first render: the alert paints, the report effect (which reads the render values) calls onError with the old error, and the retry that ensureDocumentLoad starts later fires onLoad on success.
- Documented intent: forgetFailure TSDoc (cache.ts:282-285): "When the last holder leaves, the record goes, so a new mount reads the document as not loaded: it paints no stale error on its first frame, and its hook reports only what its own retry settles." Tests cover only an unsubscribe after the failure (pdf-viewer-document-cache.test.ts:313, use-pdf-viewer-report.test.ts:211), not during the load.
- Reach: shipped. packages/ui/src/docs/pages/components/pdf-viewer/playground.tsx:5-7 (a `src` viewer on an external URL; a failed fetch with a navigation away mid-load and back reaches it; it passes no onError). No other root mounts a `src` viewer (apps and modules: none; other docs examples pass `pages`).
- Severity: low. The stale alert shows for one frame and heals when the retry publishes; no shipped consumer passes onError/onLoad, so the double report has no shipped listener.
- Kind: bug
- Prior art: NONE (searched digest for PdfViewer, onError, cache, failure)

- Severity: low. Needs two rare events in sequence, and the stray click clears the flag, so a reopen works.
- Kind: correctness
- Prior art: NONE (S5 covers useTouchTap, not the backdrop flag)

- File: `packages/ui/src/utilities/children.ts` (flattenFragments / partitionByType)
- Claim: Both elements get the same flattened key (`$<id>`), so the list that `partitionByType` returns holds duplicate keys. React warns about duplicate keys and can reuse or drop the wrong element on reorder. React itself keys each array in its own namespace, so the same children are valid when rendered directly.

- Trigger: Children.forEach walks nested arrays into one flat index and passes each element with its own key, so two arrays (or an array and a direct child) with the same element key both give `$<key>`; partitionByType then clones both with the same key.

### Q2-C09

- File: `packages/ui/src/modules/form/form-error-summary.tsx` (FormErrorSummary)
- Claim: The summary shows again (with the new row) although no submit failed, because `open` stays true after the last row went; it does not take focus this time.
- Mechanism:
  - `packages/ui/src/modules/form/form-error-summary.tsx:115-119 if (failures !== seen.failures || resets !== seen.resets) { ... setOpen(failures !== seen.failures)`
  - `packages/ui/src/modules/form/form-error-summary.tsx:128 const visible = open && items.length > 0`
- Trigger: After a failed submit under 'touched' or 'change', the user fixes each listed field, so items reaches 0 and the summary goes, but `open` stays true. A later edit that makes a field invalid again gives items > 0, and the summary shows again with no new failure (focusPending is false, so it does not take the focus).
- Documented intent: form-error-summary.tsx:70 "A list of the errors of the enclosing `Form`, which shows after a submit fails" and :76-77 "A row goes when its field becomes valid, and the summary goes with the last row." The reset test form-error-summary.test.tsx:379 states the principle "A validation after the reset gives an issue, but no submit failed." and expects the summary to stay closed.
- Reach: none. NONE. The one consumer, packages/ui/src/docs/pages/modules/form/error-summary.tsx:20, sets validateOn="submit", so no row goes without a submit. Searched the roots for FormErrorSummary. Rule R19 applies.
- Severity: low. No consumer root pairs the summary with 'touched' or 'change' validation, so the trigger stays at low per R19.
- Kind: correctness
- Prior art: R19 (quoted under Q2-C03).

- Documented intent: CONVENTIONS §5.10 "A fill or a bar that shows a value or a selection takes `mark`."; kata/progress.ts:23 base: ['h-full', rounded.full, forced.mark] for the same kind of fill.
- Reach: shipped. packages/ui/src/docs/pages/components/password-strength/*.tsx (custom-rules, minimum-strength, with-input), password-input/validation.tsx, password-confirm/with-strength-meter.tsx, modules/form/validation.tsx, under forced colors.
- Severity: low. The bar is aria-hidden and decorative, and the visible label and the native meter still give the level.
- Kind: correctness
- Prior art: NONE (digest searched for forced, password)

### R1-C16

- File: `packages/ui/src/recipes/kata/grid-group.ts` (k.rule)
- Claim: The 2px colored underline rule of the group band is a background only and disappears.

  - `packages/ui/src/recipes/kata/grid-group.ts:28 zinc: 'bg-zinc-600',`

- Reach: shipped. packages/ui/src/docs/pages/modules/grid/groups/column-groups.tsx, collapsible-groups.tsx, group-editor.tsx under forced colors.

- Prior art: NONE (digest searched for forced, grid group, band)

## Root-cause groups

- **M1**: ChartContextMenu keeps its dialog open state (`open`, `dataOpen`) across a render path that unmounts the dialog, so no close runs and the dialog reopens when the path returns. Members: C2-C10, C2-C11.
- **M2**: The reference-rule tooltip swatch is a hand-built span painted by background alone at a raw 2 px height, where the legend chip of the same rule uses the Swatch component. Members: C2-C18, C2-C19.
- **M3**: chartChrome places the touch menu button by whether a header exists (`corner: head ? null`), not by whether an inline header shows, and the public contextMenu TSDoc states a third rule. Members: C2-C06, C2-C08.
- **M4**: ChartLegend toggleReference writes the reference recede on a rule's return directly, bypassing the `live` drawn check and assuming a pointer or focus rests on the chip. Members: C2-C02, C2-C03.
- **M5**: resolveAxes returns `config: {}` under `axes={false}` (chart-axes/schema.ts:151), and both the cartesian buildGrid and the scatter-chart grid flags read only that config, never the draw flag, so the gridlines draw in a bare-marks chart. Members: C1-C10, C3-C04.
- **M6**: GridBooleanEditInput destructures none of `error`, `errorId`, `cancel`, `managed`, so the yes/no editor neither links its error nor keeps Escape under a consumer-owned session. Members: G1-C02, G1-C03.
- **M7**: The new-row slot hands its editors a draft row built by readDraftRow (field-keyed, blanks kept) that differs from the add's `values` and doubles as the cancel target of GridCellEditor. Members: G1-C04, G1-C05.
- **M8**: useGridTable calls resetPage after every global or column filter set, without checking whether the row set can change (highlight mode, or a structural no-op). Members: G3-C01, G3-C02.
- **M9**: The row shell keys 'clickable' on four handlers for focus and the class, but on onRowClick alone for data-clickable and for the keyboard activation, and the cell activation ignores the double-click handlers. Members: G4-C02, G4-C03.
- **M10**: applyRowKeyOrder (grid-group/row-manager.ts) builds a new array on each call and keeps a repeated key, and GridBody passes that output straight to GridGroupedCursorOrder and the grouped body (grid-body.tsx:338), so the memo misses on each render and a repeated key shows a group twice. Members: G4-C08, G5-C08.
- **M11**: The aggregate cells (GridAggregateCells) and the group-total reveal cells (GroupRevealCell) render without pinnedCellProps, so a frozen column's figure on group-header, group-total, and grand-total rows has no sticky class, inset, or data-grid-pin. Members: G5-C01, G5-C02.
- **M12**: collapsedHiddenIds and the collapse seed in useGridGroup read a group's raw columns list and defaultCollapsed, ignoring the collapsible flag and the first-claim binding of groupByColumn. Members: G5-C03, G5-C04.
- **M13**: A tabbable test that matches FOCUSABLE_SELECTOR (utilities/focusable-selector.ts) counts a control the browser skips: useA11yHasTabbable stops at the bare selector (hidden inputs, fieldset-disabled controls), and getTabbableElements does not drop a control that is not rendered or is inert. Members: H1-C02, P2-C04.
- **M14**: Key handlers act on a range or commit key without the hasBrowserModifier test, so they take browser chords. Members: K3-C07, K3-C08.
- **M15**: Barreled props types of components that render an element declare their own keys instead of a ComponentProps base, and the props-base gate misses them because each declares `id` or renders through a component root. Members: K3-C10, K3-C11, K3-C12, K3-C13.
- **M16**: The card fields call onValidityChange (and onBrandChange) on each input event, not only when the masked value changes. Members: K3-C15, K3-C16, K3-C17.
- **M17**: A setter reports a value without comparing it with the value it holds, so a no-op step emits a change. Members: K4-C05, K4-C06.
- **M18**: A control resolves its read-only state outside useControlProps, so the Form lock (and for FileUpload, a read-only Control) does not reach it. Members: K3-C03, K4-C01.
- **M19**: A ref flag that a timer or a settled promise clears survives an Activity hide, while the effect cleanup removes the timer that would clear it. Members: K4-C03, K4-C04.
- **M20**: Controls on useFormValue (directly or through useControlPickerField) never attach resetRef, so a native form reset does not restore an uncontrolled value. Members: K4-C09, K4-C10.
- **M21**: Alert ties the focus return to a press flag that only a later closed commit of the same instance reads and clears, so an unmount skips it and a refused press leaves it armed. Members: K7-C01, K7-C02.
- **M22**: TSDoc or comment sentences state behavior the code deliberately does not have, while a sibling TSDoc or a test states the real behavior. Members: M1-C08, M1-C09, M1-C15, M2-C11, M2-C12, M2-C14.
- **M23**: The OSRM client maps failures through assumptions (a 200 refusal, an AbortError reason, an object body, a valid timeout) that a server or a caller can break, so fetchOsrmRoute misclassifies or rejects instead of answering { ok: false, failure }. Members: M1-C01, M1-C02, M1-C03, M1-C04.
- **M24**: Only MapPoint reads the plat's dot pool; MapPoints and MapMarker size their targets from their own dots, so the pool divides ground on one side of a pair only. Members: M2-C05, M2-C06.
- **M25**: The hover provider's scroll-settle path is gated on the tooltip flag alone and resolves regions off the DOM with no check of the region layer's pointer state. Members: M2-C02, M2-C13.
- **M26**: Two parts outside the shared trigger and field primitives write their own root element and omit the §3.11 state attributes: EventsButton stamps no data-open for the sheet it opens, and the QueryChips row stamps no data-disabled. Members: Q1-C10, Q1-C11.
- **M27**: Recipe prose states size numbers from an earlier geometry, and the density ramps in dan/size.ts and core/density/geometry.ts have since moved to the five-step text-coupled formulas. Members: R1-C02, R1-C03, R1-C04, R1-C05, R1-C07, R1-C08.
- **M28**: A part that shows a state or an edge only with a background has no sen.forced entry, so forced colors remove it (CONVENTIONS §5.10), and recipes/forced-colors.test.ts lists none of these parts. Members: R1-C15, R1-C16, R1-C17, R1-C19, R1-C20.
- **M29**: A fixed spacing class that a comment ties to a stepped value (a gap or a control height) is right only at md. Members: R1-C10, R1-C11.

## Recommended resolution

The steps run in this order: the medium steps first, then the low steps; within a band, a step that another step needs goes first. Steps that share a file but not a symbol go on one branch: `use-grid-table.ts` (S15, S68), `grid-body.tsx` (S18, S74, S75), `range-legend.tsx` (S10, S54).
